Construction of f-categories and f-liftings

Proposition A.1.9
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category,
\DF
be an f-category over
\mathcal{D}
, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be a full triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{D}
that is stable by isomorphisms. We define a full
subcategory
\DF^{\prime}
of
\DF
by

\Ob\DF^{\prime}=\{K\in\Ob\mathcal{D}\mid\forall r\in\mathbb{Z},\ \Gr^{r}K\in\Ob\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\}.

Then
\DF^{\prime}
is a triangulated subcategory of
\DF
, it is stable by isomorphisms, we have
s(\DF^{\prime})\subset\DF^{\prime}
and
i(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\subset\DF^{\prime}
. The data of

\DF^{\prime}
,
\DF^{\prime}\cap\DF(\leq 0)
,
\DF^{\prime}\cap\DF(\geq 0)
,
s_{\mid\DF^{\prime}}:\DF^{\prime}\longrightarrow\DF^{\prime}
,
\alpha
and
i_{\mid\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\DF^{\prime}
defines an f-category over
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

As the functors
\Gr^{r}
are triangulated,
\DF^{\prime}
is a triangulated subcategory of
\DF
; it is clearly stable by isomorphisms, and it stable by
s
thanks to the isomorphisms

\Gr^{r}\circ s=\Gr^{r-1}
(Proposition
A.1.5
(i)).
If
X\in\Ob\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, then
\Gr^{r}(i(X))=0
if
r\neq 0
and
\Gr^{0}(i(X))\simeq X
(Proposition
A.1.5
(ii)), so
i(X)\in\Ob\DF^{\prime}
. To prove the last assertion, we check the conditions of
Definition
A.1.1
. Conditions (i)-(iii) are clear. To check condition (iv), it suffices by Proposition
A.1.3
(iii) to prove that the functors

\sigma_{\leq n}
,
\sigma_{\geq n}
preserve
\DF^{\prime}
; but this follows immediately from Proposition
A.1.5
(iii).
∎

The next proposition, which follows easily from the definitions, is used to construct an f-category over the triangulated category of horizontal constructible complexes
in Section
2.4
.

Proposition A.1.10
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a small category, let
(\DF_{x})_{x\in\Ob\mathcal{U}}
be an inductive system of f-categories where all transition functors are f-functors, let
(\mathcal{D}_{x})_{x\in\Ob\mathcal{U}}
be
an inductive system of triangulated categories where all transition functors are triangulated functors. Suppose that, for every
x\in\Ob\mathcal{U}
, we have a functor

i_{x}:\mathcal{D}_{x}\longrightarrow\DF_{x}
making
\DF_{x}
an f-lifting of
\mathcal{D}_{x}
and that, for every morphism
x\longrightarrow y
of
\mathcal{U}
, the diagram

\textstyle{\DF_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\DF_{y}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{x}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{y}}

commutes up to isomorphism; we also suppose that these isomorphisms are compatible with the composition of morphisms of
\mathcal{U}
.

Then
\DF:=2-\varinjlim_{x\in\Ob\mathcal{U}}\DF_{x}
is an f-category over
\mathcal{D}:=2-\varinjlim_{x\in\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{D}_{x}
and, for every
x\in\Ob\mathcal{U}
, the canonical functor
\DF_{x}\longrightarrow\DF
is an f-lifting
of the canonical functor
\mathcal{D}_{x}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
.

Our main source of f-liftings will be filtered derived functors, so we recall how they are constructed.
Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be abelian categories, and let
T:\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be a left exact functor.
If
(A,F^{\bullet})
is an object of
\Filf(\mathcal{A})
, we define a filtration
F^{\bullet}
on
T(A)
by setting
F^{i}(T(A))=\mathrm{Im}(T(F^{i}A)\longrightarrow T(A))
for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
; in
particular, we have canonical morphisms
T(F^{i}A)\longrightarrow F^{i}(T(A))
, hence also
T(\Gr^{i}(A))\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}(T(A))
(as
T
is left exact, we have

T(\Gr^{i}(A))\simeq T(F^{i}(A))/T(F^{i+1}(A))
).
This defines an additive functor
\Filf(\mathcal{A})\longrightarrow\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
(the action on morphisms is the restriction of that of
T
), that we denote by
\Filf(T)
.

If
\Filf(T)
admits a right derived functor
R\Filf(T)
in the sense of Definition 1.3.1 of Schneiders’s book
[
Sch98
]
, we see that
R\Filf(T)
is the

filtered right derived functor
of
F
.

8
8

8

The definition of a right derived functor in this setting is analogous to the usual one:
a right derived functor of
\Filf(T)
is a left Kan extension of
\Ko^{+}(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Ko^{+}(\Filf(T))}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Ko^{+}(\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))\longrightarrow\D^{+}(\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))

along the canonical functor
\Ko^{+}(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))\longrightarrow\D^{+}(\Fil(\mathcal{A}))
.

Proposition A.1.11
.

Let
T:\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be a left exact functor between abelian categories.
If
\mathcal{A}
has a
T
-injective subcategory in the
sense of Definition 13.3.4 of
[
KS06
]
, then
\Filf(T):\Filf(\mathcal{A})\longrightarrow\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
has a right derived functor,
and the squares

\textstyle{\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\Filf(T)}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\mathcal{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{RF}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})}

\textstyle{\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\Filf(T)}
\scriptstyle{\Gr}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gr}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\mathcal{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{RT}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})}

\textstyle{\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\Filf(T)}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\leq i}}
\textstyle{\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\leq i}}
\textstyle{\DFpl(\mathcal{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{RT}
\textstyle{\DFpl(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})}

commute up to natural isomorphism.

In particular, if
RT
sends
\Db(\mathcal{A})
to
\Db(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
, then
R\Filf(T)
sends
\DFb(\mathcal{A})
to
\DFb(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
, because
\DFb(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
is the full subcategory of
\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))
whose
objects are the
K
such that
\Gr(K)
is in
\Db(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
. In that case,
R\Filf(T)
will be an f-lifting of
RT
.

Before we prove the proposition, we introduce some definitions.
We assume that
\mathcal{A}
has a
T
-injective subcategory
\mathcal{I}
. By Proposition 13.3.5 of
[
KS06
]
, this implies that
T
has a right derived functor
RT:\D^{+}(\mathcal{A})\longrightarrow\D^{+}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
;
we say that an object
A
of
\mathcal{A}
is
T
-acyclic
if
R^{r}T(A)=0
for every
r\geq 1
. Then every object of
\mathcal{I}
is
T
-acyclic (Remark 13.3.6(ii) of
[
KS06
]
), so we may assume
that
\mathcal{I}
is the full subcategory of
T
-acyclic objects. The hypothesis on
\mathcal{A}
implies in particular that every object of
\mathcal{A}
injects into a
T
-acyclic object.

We say that an object
X
of
\Filf(\mathcal{A})
is
filtered
T
-acyclic
if
\Gr^{i}(X)
is
T
-acyclic for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
; we denote the full subcategory of filtered
T
-acyclic objects
by
\Filf(\mathcal{I})
. Let
X\in\Filf(\mathcal{A})
be filtered
T
-acyclic; as
T
-acyclic objects are stable by extensions and by taking
cokernels of monomorphisms, and as the filtration on
X
is finite, the object
\Fil^{i}(X)/\Fil^{j}(X)
is
T
-acyclic for all
i\leq j
in
\mathbb{Z}
, and in particular all
\Fil^{i}(X)
and
X
itself are

T
-acyclic.

Lemma A.1.12
.

(i)

For every
X\in\Ob\Filf(\mathcal{A})
, there exists a strict monomorphism
X\longrightarrow Y
with
Y
filtered
T
-acyclic.

(ii)

Let
0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0
be a strictly exact sequence in
\Filf(\mathcal{A})
. If
X
and
Y
are filtered
T
-acyclic, then so is
Z
.

(iii)

Let
X\in\Ob(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))
be filtered
T
-acyclic. Then, for every

i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the canonical morphisms
T(\Fil^{i}(X))\longrightarrow\Fil^{i}(T(X))
and
T(\Gr^{i}(X))\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}(T(X))
are isomorphisms.

(iv)

Let
0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0
be a strictly exact sequence in
\Filf(\mathcal{A})
. If
X
,
Y
and
Z
are filtered
T
-acyclic, then the sequence

0\longrightarrow\Filf(T)(X)\longrightarrow\Filf(T)(Y)\longrightarrow\Filf(T)(Z)\longrightarrow 0
is strictly exact.

Proof.

Point (i) is proved exactly like
[
Sta24
,
Lemma 05TS
]
.

Let
0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0
be an exact sequence in
\Filf(\mathcal{A})
. Then it is strictly exact if and only if the sequence
0\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}(X)\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}(Y)\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}(Z)\longrightarrow 0
is exact for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
; as

T
-acyclic objects are stable by taking cokernels of monomorphisms, this gives (ii).
Point (iv) also follows from this observation and from point (iii), as
T
sends short exact sequences of

T
-acyclic objects to short exact sequences.

It remains to prove (iii). So suppose that
X\in\Ob(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))
is filtered
T
-acyclic. By the paragraph before the statement of the lemmas, this implies
that all the
\Fil^{i}(X)
,
X/\Fil^{i}(X)
and
\Gr^{i}(X)
(and in particular
X
itself) are
T
-acyclic.
In particular, if we apply
T
to the short exact sequence of
T
-acyclic objects

0\longrightarrow\Fil^{i}(X)\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow X/\Fil^{i}(X)\longrightarrow 0,

we get a short exact sequence; this implies that
T(F^{i}(X))\longrightarrow T(X)
is injective, whence the first assertion of (iii). Now we apply
T
to the short
exact sequence of
T
-acyclic objects

0\longrightarrow\Fil^{i+1}(X)\longrightarrow\Fil^{i}(X)\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}(X)\longrightarrow 0;

this gives again a short exact sequence, so we get that

T(\Gr^{i}(X))=\Coker(T(\Fil^{i+1}(X))\longrightarrow T(\Fil^{i}(X))).

This, together with the first assertion of (iii), gives the second assertion of (iii).
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.1.11
.

In order to prove that
\Filf(T)
has a right derived functor, it suffices by Proposition 1.3.4 of
[
Sch98
]
to show that
\Filf(\mathcal{I})
is a
\Filf(T)
-injective subcategory of

\Filf(\mathcal{A})
in the sense of Definition 1.3.2 of
[
Sch98
]
; but this is exactly points (i), (ii) of (iv) of Lemma
A.1.12
.
This also gives a way to compute the right derived functor: for every
K\in\Dpl(\Filf(\mathcal{A}))
, there exists be a
filtered quasi-isomorphism (i.e. a morphism
\alpha
of complexes of
\Filf(\mathcal{A})
such that
\Gr(\alpha)
is a quasi-isomorphism)
\alpha:K\longrightarrow I
with
I
a complex of
filtered
T
-acyclic objects, and we have
R\Filf(T)(K)=\Filf(T)(I)
.
As the complexes
\omega(I)
,
\Gr(I)
and
\sigma_{\leq i}(I)=\Fil^{i}(I)
are bounded below complexes of
T
-acyclic objects of
\mathcal{A}
,
and as
\omega(\alpha)
,
\Gr(\alpha)
and
\sigma_{\leq i}(\alpha)
are quasi-isomorphisms by
[
Sta24
,
Lemma 05S3
]
, we also
have
RF(\omega(I))=F(\omega(I))=\omega(F(I))
,
RF(\Gr(I))=F(\Gr(I))\simeq\Gr(F(I))
and
RF(\sigma_{\leq i}(I))=F(\sigma_{\leq i}(I))\simeq\sigma_{\leq i}(F(I))

(the last isomorphisms are given by point (iii) of Lemma
A.1.12
). This gives the
commutativity of the three squares.
∎

A.2
t-structures and the realization functor

We review the construction of the realization functor from
[
BBD82
]
3.1 and Appendix A of
[
Beĭ87b
]
.

Definition A.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category and
\DF
be an f-category over
\mathcal{D}
. Suppose that we are given
a t-structure
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})
on
\mathcal{D}
and a t-structure
(\DF^{\leq 0},\DF^{\geq 0})
on
\DF
.
We say that these t-structures are
compatible
if
i:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\DF
is t-exact and if
s(\DF^{\leq 0})=\DF^{\leq-1}
.

Proposition A.2.2

(Proposition A.5 of
[
Beĭ87b
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category and
\DF
be an f-category over
\mathcal{D}
. Suppose that we are given
a t-structure
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})
on
\mathcal{D}
. Then there exists a unique t-structure on
\DF
compatible
with
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})
, and it is given by

\Ob\DF^{\leq 0}=\{X\in\Ob\DF\mid\forall i\in\mathbb{Z},\ \Gr^{i}X[i]\in\Ob\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}\}

\Ob\DF^{\geq 0}=\{X\in\Ob\DF\mid\forall i\in\mathbb{Z},\ \Gr^{i}X[i]\in\Ob\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}\}.

Theorem A.2.3

(Proposition A.5 and A.6 of
[
Beĭ87b
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a triangulated category and
\DF
be an f-category over
\mathcal{D}
. Suppose that we are given compatible
t-structures
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})
on
\mathcal{D}
and
(\DF^{\leq 0},\DF^{\geq 0})
on
\DF
. Denote by

H:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}:=\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}\cap\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}
the cohomology functor. We define a functor

H_{F}:\DF\longrightarrow\Cb(\mathcal{C})
in the following way: if
X\in\Ob\DF
, we set
H_{F}(X)^{i}=H^{i}\Gr^{i}(X)
, and we
take as differential
H_{F}(X)^{i}\longrightarrow H_{F}(X)^{i+1}
the map induced from the connection morphism in the
distinguished triangle
\omega(\sigma_{\leq i+1}\sigma_{\geq i+1}(X))\longrightarrow\omega(\sigma_{\leq i+1}\sigma_{\geq i}(X))\longrightarrow\omega(\sigma_{\leq i}\sigma_{\geq i}(X))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}
.

(i)

The functor
H_{F}
is well-defined, its restriction to the heart
\mathcal{C}_{F}
of
(\DF^{\leq 0},\DF^{\geq 0})
is an equivalence
of categories
G:\mathcal{C}_{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Cb(\mathcal{C})
, and
G^{-1}\circ H_{F}:\DF\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{F}
is the cohomology functor of the t-structure

(\DF^{\leq 0},\DF^{\geq 0})
.

(ii)

The functor
\omega\circ G^{-1}:\Cb(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
factors through
\Db(\mathcal{C})
.

Definition A.2.4
.

In the situation of Theorem
A.2.3
, we call the functor
\Db(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
induced by
\omega\circ G^{-1}
the

realization functor
and denote it by
\mathrm{real}
.

Example A.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian category,
let
\mathcal{D}
be a full
triangulated subcategory of
\Db(\mathcal{A})
, let
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})

be a t-structure on
\mathcal{D}
, and denote its heart by
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
\DF
be the full subcategory of
K
in
\DFb(\mathcal{A})
such that

\Gr^{i}K\in\Ob\mathcal{D}
for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. This is an f-category over
\mathcal{D}
by
Proposition
A.1.9
.
By Proposition
A.2.2
, the t-structure of
\mathcal{D}

also lifts to a compatible t-structure
(\DF^{\leq 0},\DF^{\geq 0})
on
\DF
.
The heart of this t-structure is the abelian category
with objects

\{K\in\Ob\DFb(\mathcal{A})|\forall i\in\mathbb{Z},\ \Gr^{i}K[i]\in\Ob\mathcal{C}\}.

It is the category called “
\mathcal{D}F_{\text{b\^{e}te}}
” in

[
BBD82
]
3.1.7.
If
(K,F^{\bullet})
is an object of this category, then the sequence

\ldots\longrightarrow\Gr^{i}K[i]\longrightarrow\Gr^{i+1}K[i+1]\longrightarrow\Gr^{i+2}K[i+2]\longrightarrow\ldots

is a bounded complex of objects of
\mathcal{C}
, which is the image of
(K,F^{\bullet})
by the
functor
G
of Theorem
A.2.3
.

A.3
The realization functor and f-liftings

Now we come to the second goal of this subsection. We start with some preliminaries.
Let
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be triangulated categories and
T:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be a triangulated functor. Suppose that we are given a t-structure
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})
(resp.
({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}^{\leq 0},{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}^{\geq 0})
)
on
\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
), and denote the cohomology functors of this t-structure by
\mathrm{H}^{i}
and its heart by
\mathcal{C}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
).
We say that an object
X
of
\mathcal{C}
is
T
-acyclic
if
T(X)\in\Ob\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. If
X
is
T
-acyclic, then we have
\mathrm{H}^{n}T(X)=0
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
;
the converse if true if the t-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is nondegenerate.

Lemma A.3.1
.

(i)

The full subcategory of
T
-acyclic objects of
\mathcal{C}
is stable by extensions.

(ii)

Let
0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0
be an exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
. If
X,Y,Z
are
T
-acyclic, then
0\longrightarrow T(X)\longrightarrow T(Y)\longrightarrow T(Z)\longrightarrow 0

is an exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

(iii)

Let
(X^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
be a complex of objects of
\mathcal{C}
and let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. If
X^{k}
is
T
-acyclic and
\mathrm{H}^{k+1}(X^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})=0
, then we have

\mathrm{H}^{r}T(\Ker d^{k+1})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r+1}T(\Ker d^{k})
for every
r\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{-1,0\}
.

(iv)

Suppose that the t-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is non-degenerate.
Let
(X^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
be an exact complex of
T
-acyclic objects. Suppose that at least one of the following conditions hold:

(a)

The complex
(X^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
is bounded.

(b)

There exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
T(X)\in{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}^{[-N,N]}
for every
X\in\Ob\mathcal{C}
.

Then the complex
T(X^{\bullet})
of objects of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is exact.

(v)

Suppose that the t-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is non-degenerate and
that there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
T(X)\in{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}^{[-N,N]}
for every
X\in\Ob\mathcal{C}
.
Let
(X^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
be a complex of
T
-acyclic objects of
\mathcal{C}
. If
X^{\bullet}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex, then, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
big enough,
the complexes
\tau_{\leq n}X^{\bullet}
,
\tau_{\geq-n}X^{\bullet}
and
\tau_{\leq n}\tau_{\geq-n}X^{\bullet}
are complexes of
T
-acyclic objects, and all the maps in the square

\textstyle{\tau_{\leq n}X^{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq n}\tau_{\geq-n}X^{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{\geq-n}X^{\bullet}}

are quasi-isomorphisms. In particular,
X^{\bullet}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded
complex of
T
-acyclic objects.

Proof.

We repeatedly use the fact that a complex
X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow Z
in
\mathcal{C}
is a short exact sequence if and only if can completed to a distinguished triangle of
\mathcal{D}

(see Theorem 1.3.6 of
[
BBD82
]
).

Let
0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0
be an exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
. If
X,Z
are
T
-acyclic, then we have an exact triangle
T(X)\longrightarrow T(Y)\longrightarrow T(Z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}
with

T(X),T(Z)
in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, so
T(Y)
is in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and the sequence
0\longrightarrow T(X)\longrightarrow T(Y)\longrightarrow T(Z)\longrightarrow 0
is exact in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. This proves (i) and (ii).

In the situation of (iii), we have an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\Ker d^{k}\longrightarrow X^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d^{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{Im}(d^{k})=\Ker(d^{k+1})\longrightarrow 0,

hence a distinguished triangle
T(\Ker d^{k})\longrightarrow T(X^{k})\longrightarrow T(\Ker d^{k+1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}
.
As
\mathrm{H}^{r}T(X^{k})=0
for
r\neq 0
, the conclusion of (iii) follows from the long exact cohomology sequence of this triangle.

Suppose that we are in the situation of (iv). Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and let
r
be a positive integer. By (iii), we have isomorphisms

\mathrm{H}^{r}T(\Ker d^{k})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r+l}T(\Ker d^{k-l})
and
\mathrm{H}^{-r}T(\Ker d^{k})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{-r-l}T(\Ker d^{k+l})
for every
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Also, we have
\mathrm{H}^{r+l}T(\Ker d^{k-l})=0
and
\mathrm{H}^{-r-l}T(\Ker d^{k+l})=0

for
l
big enough; indeed, if (a) holds, this is true because
X^{k+l}=0
and
X^{k-l}=0
for
l
big enough, and if (b) holds, this is true as soon as
l\geq N
. We deduce that
\mathrm{H}^{r}T(\Ker d^{k})=0

and
\mathrm{H}^{-r}(\Ker d^{k})=0
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and every positive integer
r
, hence that all
\Ker d^{k}
are
T
-acyclic. The conclusion of (iv) then follows by applying (ii) to the short exact
sequences
0\longrightarrow\Ker d^{k}\longrightarrow X^{k}\longrightarrow\Ker d^{k+1}\longrightarrow 0
.

Finally, suppose that we are in the situation of (v). As
X^{\bullet}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex, there exists
M\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathrm{H}^{r}(X^{\bullet})=0
for
r\not\in[-M,M]
.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. If
k\geq M
and
r
is a positive integer, then we have by (iii):

\mathrm{H}^{-r}T(\Ker d^{k})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{-r-N}T(\Ker d^{k+N})=0

and

H^{r}T(\Ker d^{-k})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r+N}\Ker(d^{-k-N})=0.

Similarly, if
k\geq N+M
and
r
is a positive integer, then we have by (iii):

\mathrm{H}^{r}T(\Ker d^{k})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r+N}T(\Ker d^{k-N})=0

and

H^{-r}T(\Ker d^{-k})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{-r-N}\Ker(d^{-k+N})=0.

We conclude that
\Ker(d^{k})
is
T
-acyclic for
k\geq N+M
or
k\leq-N-M
. Also, if
n\leq-N-2
, then
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X^{\bullet})=0
and
\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(X^{\bullet})=0
,
hence
\Coker(d^{n-1})\simeq\Ker(d^{n+1})
. So the two statements of (v) hold for
n\geq N+M+2
.
∎

The following proposition is essentially proved in Section A.7 of
[
Beĭ87b
]
.

Proposition A.3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be triangulated categories, and let
\DF
(resp.
\DF^{\prime}
) be an f-category over
\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
).
Suppose that we are given compatible t-structures
(\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0})
and
(\DF^{\leq 0},\DF^{\geq 0})
(resp.
({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}^{\leq 0},{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}^{\geq 0})

and
({\DF^{\prime}}^{\leq 0},{\DF^{\prime}}^{\geq 0})
) on
\mathcal{D}
and
\DF
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and
\DF^{\prime}
), and denote the hearts of this t-structures
by
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}_{F}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{F}
). Suppose also that the t-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is non-degenerate.

Let
T:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be a triangulated functor. Suppose that the following conditions are satisfied:

(a)

The functor
T
admits an f-lifting
FT:\DF\longrightarrow\DF^{\prime}
.

(b)

Let
\mathcal{I}:=\{X\in\mathcal{C}\mid T(X)\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\}
be the full subcategory of
T
-acyclic objects of
\mathcal{C}
. Then the functor

\Kb(\mathcal{I})/\Nb(\mathcal{I})\longrightarrow\Db(\mathcal{C})
is an equivalence, where
\Kb(\mathcal{I})
is the category of bounded complexes of objects of
\mathcal{I}
up to
homotopy and
\Nb(\mathcal{I})
is its full subcategory of exact complexes.

Then the functor
\Kb(\mathcal{I})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Kb(T)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Kb(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Db(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
sends
\Nb(\mathcal{I})
to
0
, hence induces a functor

DT:\Db(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\Db(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, and the following diagram commutes up to natural isomorphism:

\textstyle{\Db(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{DT}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{real}}
\textstyle{\Db(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{real}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}

Proof.

The first statement follows from point (iv) of Lemma
A.3.1
.

We prove the second statement.
In Theorem
A.2.3
, we defined equivalences
G:\mathcal{C}_{F}\longrightarrow\Cb(\mathcal{C})
and
G^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{F}\longrightarrow\Cb(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
By (ii) of the same theorem, the functor
\omega\circ G^{-1}:\Cb(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\omega\circ{G^{\prime}}^{-1}:\Cb(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
) sends exact complexes to
0
, hence
induces a functor
\Db(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\Db(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
), which is the realization functor
\mathrm{real}
.
Now let
\mathcal{I}_{F}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{F}
whose objects are the
X
such that
\Gr^{i}X[i]\in\Ob\mathcal{I}
for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, i.e. such that
G(X)

is in
\Cb(\mathcal{I})
. Proposition
A.1.8
implies that
FT
sends

\mathcal{I}_{F}
to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{F}
, and that the restrictions of
G^{\prime}\circ FT
and
\Cb(T)\circ G
to
\mathcal{I}_{F}
are isomorphic. So we get an isomorphism of functors on
\Cb(\mathcal{I})
:

T\circ\omega\circ G^{-1}\simeq\omega\circ FT\circ G^{-1}\simeq\omega\circ{G^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\Cb(T).

This gives the isomorphism
T\circ\mathrm{real}\simeq\mathrm{real}\circ DT
.
∎

Remark A.3.3
.

(1)

Suppose that we are in the situation of Proposition
A.3.2
.
If moreover
T:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is left t-exact and if
\mathcal{I}
is cogenerating in
\mathcal{C}
(i.e. every object of
\mathcal{C}
has a monomorphism into an object of
\mathcal{I}
), then the functor

\mathrm{H}^{0}(T):\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
admits a right derived functor
RT:\Dpl(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\Dpl(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
by Proposition 13.3.5 of
[
KS06
]
, and the construction of
RT
in that proposition shows that

RT
sends that
\Db(\mathcal{C})
to
\Db(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
and that
DT
is the restriction of
RT
to
\Db(\mathcal{C})
. We have a similar statement if
T
is right t-exact and
\mathcal{I}
is generating in
\mathcal{C}
.

(2)

By Proposition 10.2.7 of
[
KS06
]
, to check assumption (b) in the statement of Proposition
A.3.2
, it suffices to find triangulated subcategories

\mathcal{D}_{0}=\Kb(\mathcal{C})\supset\mathcal{D}_{1}\supset\ldots\supset\mathcal{D}_{r}=\Kb(\mathcal{I})
of
\Kb(\mathcal{C})
such that, for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,r-1\}
, one of the following conditions holds:

–

For every
X\in\Ob\mathcal{D}_{i}
, there exists a quasi-isomorphism
X\longrightarrow Y
with
Y\in\Ob\mathcal{D}_{i+1}
.

–

For every
X\in\Ob\mathcal{D}_{i}
, there exists a quasi-isomorphism
Y\longrightarrow X
with
Y\in\Ob\mathcal{D}_{i+1}
.

A.4
Application to horizontal perverse sheaves

In this section, we explain how to construct the f-categories underlying the triangulated categories of the main text,
as well as f-liftings of the triangulated functors between these categories.

\ell
-adic complexes

Let
X
be a scheme and
E
be an algebraic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
.
We use the notation of Section
2.1
.

By Example
A.1.2
applied to
\mathcal{A}=\Sh(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
and Proposition
A.1.9

applied to the bounded filtered category of
\mathcal{A}
and the full subcategory
\Dbc(X,E)
of

\Dpl(\mathcal{A})
, we get an f-category
\DFbc(X,E)
over the triangulated category
\Dbc(X,E)
.

Let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a morphism of finite type.
We have triangulated
functors
f_{*}
,
\otimes
and
\Homf_{X}
on
\D^{+}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
,
\D^{-}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)\times\D(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
and
\D(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)^{\circ}\times\Dpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
,
and they are all derived functors, so we can extend them
to triangulated functors on the filtered derived categories

\DFpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
,
\DF^{-}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)\times\DF(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
and
\DF(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)^{\circ}\times\DFpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
,
using Proposition
A.1.11
.

9
9

9

For

\Homf_{X}
and
\otimes
,
we could also use V.2 of
[
Ill71
]
.

Next, if
X
has a dimension function, using the fact that

\Dpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
is equivalent to the full subcategory
of
\DFpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
with objects the
K
such that

\Gr^{i}K=0
for
i\not=0
, we can see the dualizing
complex
\widehat{K}_{X}
as an
object of
\DFpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
, and so we can define
D_{X}
on

\DF(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
by
D_{X}(K)=\Homf_{X}(K,K_{X})
. Finally, we extend
the inverse image functor. The functor
f_{*}:\Dpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)\longrightarrow\Dpl(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
has a left adjoint
f^{*}
, given by

f^{*}K=f^{*}_{\mathrm{naive}}K\otimes_{f^{*}_{\mathrm{naive}}E_{X}}E_{Y}
, where

f^{*}_{\mathrm{naive}}
is the regular pullback functor (see Remark
6.8.15 of
[
BS15
]
). The functor
f^{*}_{\mathrm{naive}}
is exact and so
extends to
\DFpl(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
by Proposition
A.1.11
, and
we can see
f^{*}_{\mathrm{naive}}E_{Y}
and
E_{X}
as objects of
\DFpl(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
,
so
f^{*}
also extends.
Restricting all these functors to the subcategories
\DFbc
, we get f-liftings of
the functors
f_{*}
,
f^{*}
,
\otimes
,
\Homf_{X}
and
D_{X}
(when
X
has a dimension function
for the last one) on the categories
\Dbc
.

Perverse t-structure

Fix a scheme
X
as in section
2.2
.
Applying Proposition
A.2.2

to the f-lifting
\DFc(X,E)
of the triangulated category
\Dbc(X,E)

and to the perverse t-structure on
\Dbc(X,E)
,
we get a compatible t-structure on
\DFbc(X,E)
.
Then Theorem
A.2.3
gives a triangulated realization functor

\mathrm{real}:\Db\Perv(X,E)\longrightarrow\Dbc(X,E)
extending the inclusion
\Perv(X,E)\subset\Dbc(X,E)
.

We can apply Proposition
A.3.2
and Remark
A.3.3
to any functor between
categories
\Dbc(X,E)
that is t-exact for the perverse t-structures and constructed from
the 6 operations
f_{*},f^{*},f_{!},f^{!},\otimes,\Homf_{X}
.

For example,
if
Y
is a scheme satisfying the same conditions as
X
, and if

T=f_{*}
or
T=f_{!}
for
f:X\to Y
quasi-finite affine (resp.

T=f^{*}[d]
for
f:Y\to X
smooth of relative dimension
d
), then
we get the
commutative diagrams of point (i) (resp. (ii)) of Proposition
2.3.1
.
Similarly, taking for
T=D_{X}:\Dbc(X,E)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\Dbc(X,E)
the duality functor, we
get point (iii) of the same Proposition, and taking
T
to be an appropriate shift of
the restriction to the generic fiber functor, we get Proposition
2.3.2
).

Horizontal constructible complexes

We use the notation of Section
2.4
.
In particular,
k
is a field of finite type over its prime field,
X
is a separated
finite type
k
-scheme and
E
is an algebraic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
.

We define an f-category
\DFbh(X,E)
over
\Dbh(X,E)
using Proposition
A.1.10
.
First, for every
(A,\mathcal{X})\in\Ob\mathcal{U}X
, we get an f-category
\DFbc(\mathcal{X},E)
over
\Dbc(\mathcal{X},E)
by
applying Proposition
A.1.9
to the triangulated subcategory
\Dbc(\mathcal{X},E)
of
the bounded derived category of proétale sheaves of
E
-modules on
\mathcal{X}
.
Next, if
(A,\mathcal{X})\longrightarrow(A^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
is a morphism in
\mathcal{U}X
, then the functor

\Dbc(\mathcal{X},E)\longrightarrow\Dbc(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},E)
is the restriction of the trivial derived functor of
a functor
\Sh(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)\longrightarrow\Sh(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},E)
, so, by Proposition
A.3.2
,
it admits an f-lifting
\DFbc(\mathcal{X},E)\longrightarrow\DFbc(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},E)
.
We set

\DFbh(X,E)=2-\varinjlim_{(A,\mathcal{X})\in\Ob\mathcal{U}X}\DFbc(\mathcal{X},E);

this is an f-category over
\Dbh(X,E)
.

Moreover, if
\eta^{*}:\Dbh(X,E)\longrightarrow\Dbc(X,E)
is the exact
functor induced
by the restriction functors
\Dbc(\mathcal{X},E)\longrightarrow\Dbc(\mathcal{X}\otimes_{A}k,E)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u^{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Dbc(X,E)
, for
(A,\mathcal{X},u)\in\Ob\mathcal{U}X
, then
\eta^{*}
admits an f-lifting, by
Proposition
A.3.2
.

By Proposition
A.2.2
, the perverse t-structure on
\Dbh(X,E)
of
Section
2.5

lifts to a compatible t-structure on
\DFbh(X,E)
, so
by Theorem
A.2.3
,
we get a realization functor
\mathrm{real}:\Db\Perv_{h}(X,E)\longrightarrow\Dbh(X,E)
.

Mixed perverse sheaves

We use the notation of Section
2.6
, so
X
and
E
are as before.

Applying Proposition
A.1.9
to the f-category
\DFbh(X,E)
lifting

\Dbh(X,E)
, we get an f-category
\DFbm(X,E)
lifting
\Dbm(X,E)
.
The realization functor
\mathrm{real}:D^{b}\Perv_{h}(X,E)\longrightarrow\Dbh(X,E)
restricts to a functor

\mathrm{real}:D^{b}\Perv_{m}(X,E)\longrightarrow\Dbh(X,E),

whose essential image is contained in
\Dbm(X,E)
by
definition of
\Dbm(X,E)
; this is also the realization functor that we would
get by applying Proposition
A.2.2

and Theorem
A.2.3
to the f-category
\DFbm(X,E)
.
